\lambda_{q}^{\gamma}(\bm{\theta})=\lim_{n\to\infty}\lambda_{nq}^{\gamma}(\bm{\theta})=\infty,\hskip 9.24994pt\mathcal{L}\text{-a.e. in }\bm{\Theta}.

(B.29)

By (
B.28
) and (
B.29
), we showed that if (i) and (ii) in Theorem
4.1
hold then (iv) and (v) in Definition
4.5
hold and we can write decomposition (
11
) with
idiosyncratic component
\xi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=\delta_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}
and common component
\chi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=\gamma_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=\underline{\bm{c}}_{\ell}(L)\bm{z}_{\bm{\varsigma}}
with

\bm{c}_{\ell}\in L_{2}^{q}(\bm{\Theta},\mathbb{C})
. Because of Remark
4.4
we can always find a transformation such that we can also write
\chi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=\underline{\bm{b}}_{\ell}(L)\bm{u}_{\bm{\varsigma}}
as in (
12
) this proves part (i) and (ii) in Definition
4.5
. Finally, part (iii) in Definition
4.5
follows from orthogonality of
\gamma_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}
and
\delta_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}
in the canonical decomposition (
B.23
). This completes the proof of the sufficient condition.

B.3
Proof of Theorem
4.1
- necessary condition

From (iii) in Definition
4.5

\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})=\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})+\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta}).

Then, for all
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}
and any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, by Weyl’s inequality and since, by definition,
\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})
is positive semi-definite for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\lambda_{nq}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\geq\lambda_{nq}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})+\lambda_{nn}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})\geq\lambda_{nq}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta}).

By taking the limit for
n\to\infty
and because of (v) in Definition
4.5
, we prove (ii) in Theorem
4.1
.
Again by Weyl’s inequality, for all
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}
and any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we also have

\lambda_{n,q+1}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\leq\lambda_{n,q+1}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})+\lambda_{n1}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})=\lambda_{n1}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})

By taking the limit for
n\to\infty
and because of (iv) in Definition
4.5
, we prove (i) in Theorem
4.1
. This completes the proof of the necessary condition.

B.4
Proof of Corollary
4.2

Supposing that
\bm{x}
is a
q
-GSTFM with representation (
11
)-(
12
), as we have shown,
\bm{x}
also has the canonical representation

x_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=\gamma_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}+\delta_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}},

where
\gamma_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=\text{proj}(x_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|\mathcal{G}(\bm{x}))=\underline{\bm{c}}_{\ell}(L)\bm{z}_{\bm{\varsigma}}
and
\bm{z}_{\bm{\varsigma}}
is a
q
-dimensional orthonormal white noise rf and
\overline{\text{span}}(\bm{z})=\mathcal{G}(\bm{x})
. Since
\bm{\xi}
is idiosyncratic, we have
\mathcal{G}(\bm{x})\subseteq\overline{\text{span}}(\bm{\chi})
, which, by noting that
\overline{\text{span}}(\bm{\chi})\subseteq\overline{\text{span}}(\bm{u})
, entails
\overline{\text{span}}(\bm{z})\subseteq\overline{\text{span}}(\bm{u})
. On the other hand, since both
\bm{z}
and
\bm{u}
are
q
-dimensional white noise rf, we have
\overline{\text{span}}(\bm{z})=\overline{\text{span}}(\bm{u})
. Therefore,

\mathcal{G}(\bm{x})=\overline{\text{span}}(\bm{\chi})=\overline{\text{span}}(\bm{u}).

This implies that
\chi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}\in\mathcal{G}(\bm{x})
and
\xi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}\perp\mathcal{G}(\bm{x})
, so that
\chi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=\gamma_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=\text{\rm proj}(x_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|\mathcal{G}(\bm{x}))
and
\xi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=\delta_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}
. Uniqueness follows from uniqueness of the canonical representation.
This completes the proof.

Appendix C
Proof of results of Section 5

C.1
Proof of Theorem
5.1

The proof requires the following definition and preliminary lemmas.

Definition C.1

(Cauchy sequence of spaces)
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\bm{v}_{n}=\{\bm{v}_{n\bm{\varsigma}},\bm{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\}
be a
q
-dimensional orthonormal white noise rf such that
\bm{v}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}\in\bm{\mathcal{X}}
and is co-homostationary with
x_{\ell}
,
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, so that
\bm{v}_{n}
and
\bm{v}_{m}
are co-homostationary for any
n
and
m
.
Denote by
\bm{A}^{mn}(\bm{\theta})
the
q\times q
matrix whose
(h,k)
entry is the cross spectrum
\mathcal{S}(v_{mh\bm{\varsigma}},v_{nk\bm{\varsigma}};\bm{\theta})
, for
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}
. The orthogonal projection, element by element, of
\bm{v}_{m\bm{\varsigma}}
on the process
\bm{v}_{n}
is
\underline{\bm{A}}^{mn}(L)\bm{v}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}
.
Consider the orthogonal decomposition

\bm{v}_{m\bm{\varsigma}}=\underline{\bm{A}}^{mn}(L)\bm{v}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}+\bm{\rho}_{\bm{\varsigma}}^{mn},

(C.30)

and let
\bm{\varrho}^{mn}(\bm{\theta})
denote the spectral density matrix of
\bm{\rho}_{\bm{\varsigma}}^{mn}
. The sequence
\{\bm{v}_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}\}
generates a Cauchy sequence of spaces if, for a given
\epsilon>0
and
\mathcal{L}
-a.e. in
\bm{\Theta}
, there exists an integer
m_{\epsilon}(\bm{\theta})
such that for
n,m>m_{\epsilon}(\bm{\theta})
,
\text{\rm trace}(\bm{\varrho}^{mn}(\bm{\theta}))<\epsilon
.

Lemma C.1
.

Assume that
\{\bm{v}_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}\}
fulfills Definition
C.1
and
y=\{y_{\bm{\varsigma}},\bm{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\}
is such that
y_{\bm{\varsigma}}\in\bm{\mathcal{X}}
and is co-homostationary with
x_{\ell}
,
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
Y_{n\bm{\varsigma}}
be the orthogonal projection of
y_{\bm{\varsigma}}
on the process
\bm{v}_{n}
, i.e.,
Y_{n\bm{\varsigma}}=\text{\rm proj}(y_{\bm{\varsigma}}|\text{\rm$\overline{\text{span}}$}(\bm{v}_{n}))
. Then,
Y_{n\bm{\varsigma}}
converges in
\bm{\mathcal{X}}
in mean-square, as
n\to\infty
.

Proof.

Considering projections

y_{\bm{\varsigma}}=Y_{n\bm{\varsigma}}+r_{n\bm{\varsigma}}=\underline{\bm{b}}_{n}(L)\bm{v}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}+r_{n\bm{\varsigma}},

y_{\bm{\varsigma}}=Y_{m\bm{\varsigma}}+r_{m\bm{\varsigma}}=\underline{\bm{b}}_{m}(L)\bm{v}_{m\bm{\varsigma}}+r_{m\bm{\varsigma}},

where
{\bm{b}}_{n},{\bm{b}}_{m}\in L_{2}^{q}(\bm{\Theta},\mathbb{C})
, this yields

\underline{\bm{b}}_{n}(L)\bm{v}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}-\underline{\bm{b}}_{m}(L)\bm{v}_{m\bm{\varsigma}}=r_{m\bm{\varsigma}}-r_{n\bm{\varsigma}}.

Now we show the spectral density of the rf on the left-hand side converges to zero
\mathcal{L}
-a.e. in
\bm{\Theta}
. Note that the spectral density of the rf on left-hand side is the cross spectrum between the left- and right- hand sides, which, due to the definition of
r_{n\bm{\varsigma}}
and
r_{m\bm{\varsigma}}
, is the sum of two cross spectra:
\mathcal{S}(r_{n\bm{\varsigma}},\underline{\bm{b}}_{m}(L)\bm{v}_{m\bm{\varsigma}};\bm{\theta})+\mathcal{S}(r_{m\bm{\varsigma}},\underline{\bm{b}}_{n}(L)\bm{v}_{n\bm{\varsigma}};\bm{\theta}).
In view of (
C.30
), we have

\mathcal{S}(r_{n\bm{\varsigma}},\underline{\bm{b}}_{m}(L)\bm{v}_{m\bm{\varsigma}};\bm{\theta})=\mathcal{S}(r_{n\bm{\varsigma}},\underline{\bm{b}}_{m}(L)\star\underline{\bm{A}}^{mn}(L)\bm{v}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}+\underline{\bm{b}}_{m}(L)\bm{\rho}_{\bm{\varsigma}}^{mn};\bm{\theta})=\mathcal{S}(r_{n\bm{\varsigma}},\underline{\bm{b}}_{m}(L)\bm{\rho}_{\bm{\varsigma}}^{mn};\bm{\theta}).

Note that both the spectral density of
r_{n\bm{\varsigma}}
and the squared entries of
{\bm{b}}_{m}
are bounded in modulus by the spectral density of
y_{\bm{\varsigma}}
. Hence, the fact that
\{\bm{v}_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}\}
generates a Cauchy sequence of spaces implies that
\mathcal{S}(r_{n\bm{\varsigma}},\underline{\bm{b}}_{m}(L)\bm{\rho}_{\bm{\varsigma}}^{mn};\bm{\theta})
converges to zero
\mathcal{L}
-a.e. in
\bm{\Theta}
as
m,n\rightarrow\infty
. Similar argument holds also for
\mathcal{S}(r_{m\bm{\varsigma}},\underline{\bm{b}}_{n}(L)\bm{v}_{n\bm{\varsigma}};\bm{\theta})
. Therefore, the spectral density of
Y_{n\bm{\varsigma}}-Y_{m\bm{\varsigma}}
converges to zero
\mathcal{L}
-a.e. in
\bm{\Theta}
as
m,n\rightarrow\infty
. Since the spectral densities of
Y_{n\bm{\varsigma}}
and
Y_{m\bm{\varsigma}}
are dominated by that of
y_{\bm{\varsigma}}
, by Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem, the integral of
the spectral density of
Y_{n\bm{\varsigma}}-Y_{m\bm{\varsigma}}
converges to zero as
m,n\rightarrow\infty
, which implies that
Y_{n\bm{\varsigma}}
is a Cauchy sequence and thus converges in
\bm{\mathcal{X}}
, as
n\to\infty
.
∎

Lemma C.2
.

Suppose that (i) and (ii) of Theorem
4.1
and Assumptions
3.1
-
3.3
hold. Then,
\{{\bm{\psi}}^{n},n\in\mathbb{N}\}
, as defined in (
B.5
), generates a Cauchy sequence of spaces.

Proof.

For
n>m
, in (
B.7
), letting
\bm{C}=\bm{I}_{q}
yields

{\bm{\psi}}^{m}_{\bm{\varsigma}}=\underline{\bm{D}}(L){\bm{\psi}}^{n}_{\bm{\varsigma}}+\bm{\rho}_{\bm{\varsigma}}^{mn}.

(C.31)

where

{\bm{D}}(\bm{\theta})={\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}_{m}(\bm{\theta}){\bm{P}}_{m}(\bm{\theta}){\bm{P}}^{\dagger}_{n}(\bm{\theta}){\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}_{n}(\bm{\theta}).

Let
\bm{\varrho}^{mn}(\bm{\theta})
denote the spectral density matrix of
\bm{\rho}_{\bm{\varsigma}}^{mn}
. Lemma
B.1
and (ii) of Theorem
4.1
imply that
\text{trace}(\bm{\varrho}^{mn}(\bm{\theta}))
converges to zero
\mathcal{L}
-a.e. in
\bm{\Theta}
.

On the other hand,

{\bm{\psi}}^{n}_{\bm{\varsigma}}=\underline{\bm{D}}^{\dagger}(L){\bm{\psi}}^{m}_{\bm{\varsigma}}+\bm{\rho}_{\bm{\varsigma}}^{nm}.

(C.32)

Using (
C.31
) and (
C.32
), we have

\bm{I}_{q}={\bm{D}}(\bm{\theta}){\bm{D}}^{\dagger}(\bm{\theta})+\bm{\varrho}^{mn}(\bm{\theta})={\bm{D}}^{\dagger}(\bm{\theta}){\bm{D}}(\bm{\theta})+\bm{\varrho}^{nm}(\bm{\theta})

\mathcal{L}
-a.e. in
\bm{\Theta}
. Taking the trace on both sides and noting that the trace of
{\bm{D}}(\bm{\theta}){\bm{D}}^{\dagger}(\bm{\theta})
is equal to that of
{\bm{D}}^{\dagger}(\bm{\theta}){\bm{D}}(\bm{\theta})
, we have
\text{trace}(\bm{\varrho}^{mn}(\bm{\theta}))=\text{trace}(\bm{\varrho}^{nm}(\bm{\theta}))

\mathcal{L}
-a.e. in
\bm{\Theta}
. Finally,
\text{trace}(\bm{\varrho}^{mm}(\bm{\theta}))=0
. Therefore,
\text{trace}(\bm{\varrho}^{mn}(\bm{\theta}))
converges to zero
\mathcal{L}
-a.e. in
\bm{\Theta}
for any
m,n\rightarrow\infty
.
∎

Finally, let us consider again the orthogonal decomposition in (
B.26
) for any
\ell\leq n
, i.e.,

x_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=\underline{{\bm{\pi}}}_{n\ell}(L)\star\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}_{n}(L){\bm{\psi}}^{n}_{\bm{\varsigma}}+\underline{{\bm{q}}}_{n\ell}(L)\star\underline{{\bm{Q}}}_{n}(L)\bm{x}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}=\gamma_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{n}+\delta_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{n}.

Due to Lemma
C.1
and Lemma
C.2
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\gamma_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{n}=\gamma_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}

in mean-square and
\gamma_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}\in\bm{\mathcal{X}}
. This entails that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\delta_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{n}=x_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}-\gamma_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=\delta_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}

in mean-square, with
\delta_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}\in\bm{\mathcal{X}}
.

Moreover,
\gamma_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}
is an aggregate, i.e., it belongs to
\mathcal{G}(\bm{x})
, since
{\bm{\pi}}_{n\ell}{\bm{P}}_{n}
is a STDAS. To see this, note that the spectral density matrix of
\gamma_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{n}
, which is
{\bm{\pi}}_{n\ell}(\bm{\theta}){\bm{\Lambda}}_{n}(\bm{\theta}){\bm{\pi}}^{\dagger}_{n\ell}(\bm{\theta})
, is not smaller than
\lambda_{nq}^{x}(\bm{\theta}){\bm{\pi}}_{n\ell}(\bm{\theta}){\bm{\pi}}^{\dagger}_{n\ell}(\bm{\theta})
and it is bounded above by the spectral density of
x_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}
, call it
\sigma^{2}_{\ell}(\bm{\theta})
. Therefore, we have
{\bm{\pi}}_{n\ell}(\bm{\theta}){\bm{\pi}}^{\dagger}_{n\ell}(\bm{\theta})\leq\sigma^{2}_{\ell}(\bm{\theta})/\lambda_{nq}^{x}(\bm{\theta})
.
Assuming again without loss of generality that
\lambda_{nq}^{x}(\bm{\theta})>1
for all
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}
(see the arguments in
Forni and Lippi 2001
, Section 4.2
), it follows that
\sigma_{\ell}^{2}(\bm{\theta})/\lambda_{nq}^{x}(\bm{\theta})
is bounded above by
\sigma^{2}_{\ell}(\bm{\theta})
and it converges to zero
\mathcal{L}
-a.e. in
\bm{\Theta}
, because of (ii) in Theorem
4.1
. Hence, by Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem,
\int_{\bm{\Theta}}\sigma_{\ell}(\bm{\theta})/\lambda_{nq}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\mathrm{d}\bm{\theta}
converges to zero.

By construction,
\delta_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{n}
is orthogonal to
{\bm{\psi}}^{n}_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{h}}
for any
\bm{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}
. Since
\mathcal{G}(\bm{x})=\overline{\text{span}}(\bm{z})
by Proposition
B.6
and the process
\bm{z}
has been obtained by taking limits of linear combinations of the elements of
\bm{\psi}^{n}
, continuity of the inner product implies that
\delta_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}\perp\mathcal{G}(\bm{x})
.
Then, by uniqueness of the canonical decomposition we have that
\gamma_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=\chi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}
for all
\bm{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}
. This completes the proof.

Appendix D
Some novel results on spectral density matrix estimation for spatio-temporal random fields

In this section we derive some novel results about spectral density estimation of spatio-temporal rf. Our theory builds on and extends the one
already available in
Deb, Pourahmadi and Wu 2017
, which is available for spatial processes only, and the one of
Wu and Zaffaroni 2018
and
Wu 2005
, which study the time series case.

Consider a generic
n
-dimensional rf:

{\bm{Y}}_{n}=\{\bm{Y}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}=(Y_{1\bm{\varsigma}}\cdots Y_{n\bm{\varsigma}})^{\top},\bm{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\}
where, for any
\ell=1,\ldots,n

Y_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=F_{\ell}(\bm{\epsilon}_{\bm{\varsigma}-{\bm{k}}};{\bm{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}),

(D.33)

for some measurable function
F_{\ell}(\cdot)
a such that
Y_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}
exists and a, possibly infinite dimensional, zero-mean and i.i.d. rf
\{\bm{\epsilon}_{\bm{\varsigma}}=(\epsilon_{1\bm{\varsigma}}\ \epsilon_{2\bm{\varsigma}}\cdots)^{\top};\bm{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\}
.
Let
Y_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}\in L_{p}
, for some
p\geq 1
, and denote as
Y^{*}_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=F_{\ell}(\bm{\epsilon}^{*}_{\bm{\varsigma}-{\bm{k}}};{\bm{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\times\mathbb{N}_{0})
, with
\bm{\epsilon}^{*}_{\bm{\varsigma}}=\bm{\epsilon}_{\bm{\varsigma}}
if
\bm{\varsigma}\neq\bm{0}
and
\bm{\epsilon}^{*}_{{\bm{0}}}=\widetilde{\bm{\epsilon}}_{{\bm{0}}}
, where
\widetilde{\bm{\epsilon}}_{\bm{\varsigma}_{1}},\bm{\epsilon}_{\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}
are
i.i.d. for
\bm{\varsigma}_{1},\bm{\varsigma}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}
. Recall that we can arbitrarily set the location of the origin
\bm{0}
because of the Assumption
3.1

(homostationarity). Then, we consider the following
functional dependence measure

\delta_{\bm{\varsigma},p}^{[\ell]}=\left({\rm E}|Y_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}-Y^{*}_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|^{p}\right)^{1/p}.

(D.34)

Let also

{\bm{m}}=(m_{1}\ m_{2}\ m_{3})^{\top}
and define

\varphi_{{\bm{m}},p}^{[\ell]}=\sum_{|s_{1}|>m_{1}}\sum_{|s_{2}|>m_{2}}\sum_{t>m_{3}}\delta_{\bm{\varsigma},p}^{[\ell]}.

The definition of
\varphi_{{\bm{m}},p}^{[\ell]}
is natural in our spatio-temporal rf setting: it measures two-sided dependence over space and one-sided dependence over time for
Y_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}
. Therefore,
\varphi_{{\bm{m}},p}^{[\ell]}
considers a form of dependence which acts in each direction of
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
: this is different from the approach of
(
Deb, Pourahmadi and Wu 2017
, Definition 2.1 and see also their discussion on p.4315)
, where the dependence can be only in one spatial dimension.

Given the sample
\{Y_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}=x_{\ell(s_{1}\ s_{2}\ t)},\ \ell=1,\ldots,n,\ s_{1}=1,\ldots,S_{1},\ s_{2}=1,\ldots,S_{2},\ t=1,\ldots,T\}
, we consider the estimator of the spectral spectral density matrix
\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{y}(\bm{\theta})
of
\bm{Y}_{n}
with
(i,j)
-th,
i,j=1.\ldots,n
, generic entry

\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})=\frac{1}{S_{1}S_{2}T}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}_{1},\bm{\varsigma}_{2}=(1\ 1\ 1)^{\top}}^{(S_{1}\ S_{2}\ T)^{\top}}\!\!\!\!Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}_{1}}Y_{j\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}K_{1}\left(\frac{s_{11}-s_{21}}{B_{S_{1}}}\right)K_{2}\left(\frac{s_{12}-s_{22}}{B_{S_{2}}}\right)K_{3}\left(\frac{t_{1}-t_{2}}{B_{T}}\right)e^{-i\left\langle\bm{\varsigma}_{1}-\bm{\varsigma}_{2},\bm{\theta}\right\rangle}

(D.35)

with
\bm{\varsigma}_{1}=(s_{11}\ s_{12}\ t_{1}),\bm{\varsigma}_{2}=(s_{21}\ s_{22}\ t_{2})
,
K_{1}(\cdot),K_{2}(\cdot),K_{3}(\cdot)
being kernel functions and
B_{S_{1}},B_{S_{2}},B_{T}
being bandwidths satisfying Assumption
6.5
(i) and (ii).

The following conditions are imposed

Condition 1
.

For all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\rm E}(Y_{\ell{\bm{0}}})=0
,
Y_{\ell{\bm{0}}}\in L_{p}
, for some
p\geq 4
.

Condition 2
.

For all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,
\delta_{\bm{\varsigma},p}^{[\ell]}\leq A_{1}\rho_{1}^{|s_{1}|}\rho_{2}^{|s_{2}|}\rho_{3}^{|t|}
, for some finite
\rho_{1},\rho_{2},\rho_{3}\in(0,1)
and
A_{1}>0
, independent of
\ell
.

Condition 3
.

For all
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
,
|{\rm E}(Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}Y_{j{\bm{0}}})|\leq A_{2}\rho_{4}^{|s_{1}|}\rho_{5}^{|s_{2}|}\rho_{6}^{|t|}
, for some finite
\rho_{4},\rho_{5},\rho_{6}\in(0,1)
and
A_{2}\in(0,\infty)
, independent of
i
and
j
.

Then, we have the following result

Proposition D.1
.

Let Conditions
1
and
2
, and Assumption
6.5
hold. Then, there exists a finite
C>0
depending on
p
such that

\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\left[{\rm E}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-{\rm E}(\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta}))\right|^{p/2}\right]^{2/p}\leq C\left[\frac{(\log B_{S_{1}}\log B_{S_{2}}\log B_{T})^{2}B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}}{S_{1}S_{2}T}\right]^{1/2}.

For
p=4
, Proposition
D.1
bounds the variance of
\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})
. The following result bounds the mean square error

Proposition D.2
.

Let Conditions
1
,
2
, and
3
, and Assumption
6.5
hold. Then, there exists a finite
C^{*}>0
such that

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}{\rm E}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-\sigma_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})\right|^{2}\leq C^{*}\max\left\{\frac{(\log B_{S_{1}}\log B_{S_{2}}\log B_{T})^{2}B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}}{S_{1}S_{2}T},\frac{1}{B_{S_{1}}^{2\vartheta_{1}}},\frac{1}{B_{S_{2}}^{2\vartheta_{2}}},\frac{1}{B_{T}^{2\vartheta_{3}}}\right\}.

This implies mean-square consistency of
\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})
, as
S_{1},S_{2},T\to\infty
.

D.1
Proof of Proposition
D.1

The following preliminary result is needed to prove Proposition
D.1
.

Lemma D.3
.

For any
i=1,\ldots,n
, let
Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}\in L_{p}
for some
p>1
,
{\rm E}Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}=0
,
\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}\in\mathbb{C}
for
\bm{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}
,
A_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}=(\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}|\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}|^{2})^{1/2}
and
C_{p}=18p^{3/2}(p-1)^{-1/2}
. Then, for
\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}=(S_{1}\ S_{2}\ T)^{\top}
, we have

\left({\rm E}\left|\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}\leq C_{p}A_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\varphi_{{\bm{0}},p}^{[i]},\hskip 9.24994pt\left({\rm E}\left|\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}\widetilde{Y}_{i\bm{\varsigma}}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}\leq C_{p}A_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\varphi_{{\bm{0}},p}^{[i]}

and

\left({\rm E}\left|\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}(Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}-\widetilde{Y}_{i\bm{\varsigma}})\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}\leq C_{p}A_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\varphi_{{\bm{m}}+{\bm{1}},p}^{[i]}.

Proof.

Let
\bm{\tau}:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}^{3}
be a bijection, which is non-decreasing in the third coordinate.
Define for
l\in\mathbb{Z}
and
(\bm{\tau}(l))_{3}\leq t
, where
(\bm{\tau}(l))_{3}
means the third coordinate of
\bm{\tau}(l)
,
the projection

P_{l}(Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}})={\rm E}(Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}|\mathcal{F}_{l})-{\rm E}(Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}|\mathcal{F}_{l-1}),

where

\mathcal{F}_{l}=\sigma(\bm{\epsilon}_{\bm{\tau}(h)};h\leq l).
Define for
{\bm{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the shift
\mathcal{T}^{{\bm{k}}}\mathcal{F}_{l}=\sigma(\bm{\epsilon}_{\bm{\tau}(h)-{\bm{k}}};h\leq l).
By (
D.33
), we then have

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\left({\rm E}\left|P_{l}(Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}})\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle=\left({\rm E}\left|{\rm E}(Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}|\mathcal{F}_{l})-{\rm E}(Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}|\mathcal{F}_{l-1})\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle=\left({\rm E}\left|{\rm E}(Y_{i{\bm{0}}}|\mathcal{T}^{\bm{\varsigma}}\mathcal{F}_{l})-{\rm E}(Y_{i{\bm{0}}}|\mathcal{T}^{\bm{\varsigma}}\mathcal{F}_{l-1})\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle=\left\{{\rm E}\left|{\rm E}[F_{i}(\bm{\epsilon}_{-{\bm{k}}};{\bm{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{N}_{0})|\mathcal{T}^{\bm{\varsigma}}\mathcal{F}_{l}]-{\rm E}[F_{i}(\bm{\epsilon}_{-{\bm{k}}},\widetilde{\bm{\epsilon}}_{\bm{\tau}(l)-\bm{\varsigma}};{\bm{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}\setminus\{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\tau}(l)\})|\mathcal{T}^{\bm{\varsigma}}\mathcal{F}_{l}]\right|^{p}\right\}^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq\left({\rm E}\left|F_{i}(\bm{\epsilon}_{-{\bm{k}}};{\bm{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{N}_{0})-F_{i}(\bm{\epsilon}_{-{\bm{k}}},\widetilde{\bm{\epsilon}}_{\bm{\tau}(l)-\bm{\varsigma}};{\bm{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}\setminus\{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\tau}(l)\})\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle=\left({\rm E}\left|F_{i}(\bm{\epsilon}_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\tau}(l)-{\bm{k}}};{\bm{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{N}_{0})-F_{i}(\bm{\epsilon}_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\tau}(l)-{\bm{k}}},\widetilde{\bm{\epsilon}}_{{\bm{0}}};{\bm{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}\setminus\{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\tau}(l)\})\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle=\left({\rm E}\left|Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\tau}(l)}-Y^{*}_{i\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\tau}(l)}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\tau}(l),p}^{[i]}.

Let
\mathbb{E}_{t}=\{l\in\mathbb{Z}:(\bm{\tau}(l))_{3}\leq t\}
. Noting that
\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{E}_{T}}P_{l}(\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}})
, we have

\left({\rm E}\left|\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}=\left({\rm E}\left|\sum_{l\in\mathbb{E}_{T}}P_{l}\left(\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}\right)\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p},

where
\{P_{l}\left(\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}\right);l\in\mathbb{E}_{T}\}
forms a martingale difference sequence in time, that is,

{\rm E}\left[P_{l}\left(\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}\right)|\mathcal{F}_{l-1}\right]=0.

Hence, by Minkowski’s, Cauchy-Schwarz’s and Burkholder’s inequalities (see
Wu and Shao 2007
),

\displaystyle\left({\rm E}\left|\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}\right|^{p}\right)^{2/p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}^{2}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{E}_{T}}\left({\rm E}\left|P_{l}\left(\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}\right)\right|^{p}\right)^{2/p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}^{2}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{E}_{T}}\left(\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\left|\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}\right|\delta_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\tau}(l),p}^{[i]}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}^{2}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{E}_{T}}\left(\left(\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\left|\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}\right|^{2}\delta_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\tau}(l),p}^{[i]}\right)\left(\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\delta_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\tau}(l),p}^{[i]}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}^{2}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{E}_{T}}\left(\left(\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\left|\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}\right|^{2}\delta_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\tau}(l),p}^{[i]}\right)\varphi_{{\bm{0}},p}^{[i]}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}^{2}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}^{2}\left(\varphi_{{\bm{0}},p}^{[i]}\right)^{2},

which implies
\left({\rm E}\left|\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}\leq C_{p}A_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\varphi_{{\bm{0}},p}^{[i]}.

Repeating the similar arguments for
\widetilde{Y}_{i\bm{\varsigma}}
yields
\left({\rm E}\left|\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}\widetilde{Y}_{i\bm{\varsigma}}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}\leq C_{p}A_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\varphi_{{\bm{0}},p}^{[i]}
. Noting that

\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}(Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}-\widetilde{Y}_{i\bm{\varsigma}})=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{F}_{T}}P_{l}\left(\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\alpha_{\bm{\varsigma}}(Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}-\widetilde{Y}_{i\bm{\varsigma}})\right),

where
\mathbb{F}_{T}=\{l\in\mathbb{Z}:|\bm{\tau}(l))_{1}|>m_{1},|\bm{\tau}(l))_{2}|>m_{2},T\geq(\bm{\tau}(l))_{3}>m_{3}\}
, the last inequality follows from repeating the similar arguments as above.
∎

In the sequel,
A_{1},A_{2},...
will denote generic positive constants.
Letting

a_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}},{\bm{r}}}=K_{1}(r_{1}/B_{S_{1}})K_{2}(r_{2}/B_{S_{2}})K_{3}(r_{3}/B_{T})e^{-i\left\langle{\bm{r}},\bm{\theta}\right\rangle},

where
{\bm{r}}=(r_{1}\ r_{2}\ r_{3})^{\top}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}
, then (
D.35
) can be written as

\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})=\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}_{1}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}_{2}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}_{1}}Y_{j\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}a_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}},\bm{\varsigma}_{1}-\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}.

Assumption
6.5
implies that
\sum_{{\bm{r}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}|a_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}},{\bm{r}}}|^{2}/(B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T})<\infty
. Let
\bm{h}=(h_{1}\ h_{2}\ h_{3})^{\top}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}
and
\mathcal{R}=S_{1}S_{2}T
. Then, we consider an approximation of
\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})
by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}_{1}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}_{2}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\widetilde{Y}_{i\bm{\varsigma}_{1}}\widetilde{Y}_{j\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}a_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}},\bm{\varsigma}_{1}-\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}_{1}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\widetilde{Y}_{i\bm{\varsigma}_{1}}\widetilde{Y}_{j\bm{\varsigma}_{1}}+\frac{2}{\mathcal{R}}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}_{2}={\bm{2}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\widetilde{Y}_{j\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}\sum_{s_{11}=\max(1,s_{21}-h_{1})}^{s_{21}-1}\sum_{s_{12}=\max(1,s_{22}-h_{2})}^{s_{22}-1}\sum_{t_{1}=\max(1,t_{2}-h_{3})}^{t_{2}-1}a_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}},\bm{\varsigma}_{1}-\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}\widetilde{Y}_{i\bm{\varsigma}_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{2}{\mathcal{R}}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}_{2}={\bm{h}}+{\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\widetilde{Z}_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}_{2}},

(D.36)

where
\widetilde{Z}_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}=\widetilde{Y}_{j\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}_{1}={\bm{1}}}^{\bm{\varsigma}_{2}-{\bm{h}}}a_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}},\bm{\varsigma}_{1}-\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}\widetilde{Y}_{i\bm{\varsigma}_{1}}
.

Now let us consider the
\sigma
-field

\mathcal{F}_{{\bm{m}},\bm{\varsigma}}=\sigma(\bm{\epsilon}_{\bm{\varsigma}-{\bm{k}}};|k_{1}|\leq m_{1},|k_{2}|\leq m_{2},0\leq k_{3}\leq m_{3})

with
{\bm{k}}=(k_{1}\ k_{2}\ k_{3})^{\top}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
{\bm{m}}=(m_{1}\ m_{2}\ m_{3})^{\top}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}
.
The
\sigma
-field
\mathcal{F}_{{\bm{m}},\bm{\varsigma}}
is based on a hyperrectangle in
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
, where for each coordinate defining the spatio-temporal point
\bm{\varsigma}-{\bm{k}}
, we consider its distance form
\bm{\varsigma}
. We refer to
El Machkouri and Volnỳ 2003
p. 328 for a discussion on other options which can be considered to define this
\sigma
-field. For the sake of completeness of our argument, here we emphasize that our choice for
\mathcal{F}_{{\bm{m}},\bm{\varsigma}}
is the natural one to consider in our spatio-temporal setting, where we impose homostationarity (see Assumption
3.1
) and we are interested in controlling the rf functional dependence in each direction of
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
.

Note that
\{\widetilde{Z}_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}_{2}+l\bm{h}};l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\}
are martingale differences when
|h_{1}|>m_{1},|h_{2}|>m_{2},h_{3}>m_{3}
, that is,
{\rm E}[\widetilde{Z}_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}_{2}+l\bm{h}}|\mathcal{F}_{{\bm{m}},\bm{\varsigma}_{2}+(l-1)\bm{h}}]=0
. Taking
|h_{1}|=2m_{1},|h_{2}|=2m_{2},h_{3}=2m_{3}
and using Lemma
D.3
, there exists
A_{3}>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\left({\rm E}\left|\widetilde{Z}_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}}{(B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T})^{1/2}}

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\left({\rm E}\left|\widetilde{Y}_{j\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}\frac{\left({\rm E}\left|\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}_{1}={\bm{1}}}^{\bm{\varsigma}_{2}-{\bm{h}}}a_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}},\bm{\varsigma}_{1}-\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}\widetilde{Y}_{i\bm{\varsigma}_{1}}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}}{(B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T})^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\left({\rm E}\left|\widetilde{Y}_{j{\bm{0}}}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}C_{p}\varphi_{{\bm{0}},p}^{[i]}\left(\frac{\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}_{1}={\bm{1}}}^{\bm{\varsigma}_{2}-{\bm{h}}}|a_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}},\bm{\varsigma}_{1}-\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}|^{2}}{B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}}\right)^{1/2}\leq A_{3}

due to Conditions
1
and
2
.
For the last term in (
D.36
), we consider splitting the interval
[h_{1}+1,S_{1}]\times[h_{2}+1,S_{2}]\times[h_{3}+1,T]
into consecutive blocks, each of which has same size
2m_{1}\times 2m_{2}\times m_{3}
(here, without loss of generality, we assume
(S_{1}-h_{1}-1)/(2m_{1}),(S_{2}-h_{2}-1)/(2m_{2}),(T-h_{3}-1)/m_{3}\in\mathbb{N}
). There are
C^{*}=(S_{1}-h_{1}-1)(S_{2}-h_{2}-1)(T-h_{3}-1)/(4m_{1}m_{2}m_{3})=O(\mathcal{R}/(4m_{1}m_{2}m_{3}))
number of such blocks. In this manner, for
\bm{\varsigma}_{2}
in the non-consecutive blocks,
\{\widetilde{Z}_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}_{2}};{\bm{h}}+{\bm{1}}\leq\bm{\varsigma}_{2}\leq\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}\}
forms a martingale difference sequence. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\left({\rm E}\left|\frac{2}{\mathcal{R}}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}_{2}={\bm{h}}+{\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\widetilde{Z}_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}}{\left(m_{1}m_{2}m_{3}B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}/\mathcal{R}\right)^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\frac{4}{4m_{1}m_{2}m_{3}}\sum_{a_{1}=1}^{2m_{1}}\sum_{a_{2}=1}^{2m_{2}}\sum_{a_{3}=1}^{m_{3}}\frac{\left({\rm E}\left|\left(\frac{\mathcal{R}}{4m_{1}m_{2}m_{3}}\right)^{-1/2}\sum_{l=1}^{C^{*}}\widetilde{Z}_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}_{2}+l\bm{h}}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}}{(B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T})^{1/2}}\leq A_{4}.

(D.37)

Now, Let
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}_{1}}={\rm E}(\widetilde{Y}_{i{\bm{0}}}\widetilde{Y}_{j\bm{\varsigma}_{1}})
and consider the first two terms in (
D.36
) together in view of the fact that for any
\bm{\varsigma}_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}
,

\underset{\mathcal{R}\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\frac{\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\left({\rm E}\left|\mathcal{R}^{-1}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\widetilde{Y}_{i\bm{\varsigma}}\widetilde{Y}_{j\bm{\varsigma}+\bm{\varsigma}_{1}}-\widetilde{\Gamma}_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}_{1}}\right|^{p/2}\right)^{2/p}}{(m_{1}m_{2}m_{3}/\mathcal{R})^{1/2}}<\infty

due to the central limit theorem for
m
-dependent process (see, e.g.,
Lehmann 1999
) and Conditions
1
and
2
. Note that in the first two terms, we are combining all the terms of the form
\widetilde{Y}_{i\bm{\varsigma}_{1}}\widetilde{Y}_{j\bm{\varsigma}_{1}+{\bm{k}}}
where
|k_{1}|\leq h_{1},|k_{2}|\leq h_{2},0\leq k_{3}\leq h_{3}
. Denoting by
\widetilde{W}_{ij,\mathcal{R}}/\mathcal{R}
the last term in (
D.36
) and considering that
K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}
are bounded with support
[-1,1]
because of Assumption
6.5
, we then have

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\left({\rm E}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-\widetilde{W}_{ij,\mathcal{R}}/\mathcal{R}-{\rm E}[\widetilde{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-\widetilde{W}_{ij,\mathcal{R}}/\mathcal{R}]\right|^{p/2}\right)^{2/p}}{\left(m_{1}^{2}m_{2}^{2}m_{3}^{2}B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}/\mathcal{R}\right)^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(m_{1}m_{2}m_{3}B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T})^{1/2}}\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{2m_{1}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{2m_{2}}\sum_{k_{3}=1}^{2m_{3}}|a_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}},{\bm{k}}}|A_{5}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A_{6}\min(8m_{1}m_{2}m_{3},B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T})}{(m_{1}m_{2}m_{3}B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T})^{1/2}}A_{5}\leq A_{7}.

Now, due to (
D.37
) and the above inequality, there exists
A_{8}>0
such that

\frac{\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\left({\rm E}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-{\rm E}[\widetilde{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})]\right|^{p/2}\right)^{2/p}}{\left(m_{1}^{2}m_{2}^{2}m_{3}^{2}B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}/\mathcal{R}\right)^{1/2}}\leq A_{8}.

Define
U_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[i]}(\bm{\theta})=\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}Y_{i\bm{\varsigma}}e^{-i\left\langle\bm{\varsigma},\bm{\theta}\right\rangle}
and
\widetilde{U}_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[i]}(\bm{\theta})=\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\widetilde{Y}_{i\bm{\varsigma}}e^{-i\left\langle\bm{\varsigma},\bm{\theta}\right\rangle}
. Lemma
D.3
then implies that

\left({\rm E}\left|U_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[i]}(\bm{\theta})-\widetilde{U}_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[i]}(\bm{\theta})\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}\leq C_{p}\mathcal{R}^{1/2}\varphi_{{\bm{m}}+{\bm{1}},p}^{[i]}\ \text{and}\ \left({\rm E}\left|U_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[i]}(\bm{\theta})\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}+\left({\rm E}\left|\widetilde{U}_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[i]}(\bm{\theta})\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}\leq 2C_{p}\mathcal{R}^{1/2}\varphi_{{\bm{0}},p}^{[i]}.

Denote by
\widehat{K}_{1},\widehat{K}_{2},\widehat{K}_{3}
the Fourier transforms of
K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}
, respectively, we then have

\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})=\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\widehat{K}_{1}(u_{1})\widehat{K}_{2}(u_{2})\widehat{K}_{3}(u_{3})U_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[i]}(\bm{\theta}^{*}_{{\bm{u}}})\bar{U}_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[j]}(\bm{\theta}^{*}_{{\bm{u}}}){\rm d}{\bm{u}},

where
\bm{\theta}^{*}_{{\bm{u}}}=(B_{S_{1}}^{-1}u_{1}\ B_{S_{2}}^{-1}u_{2}\ B_{T}^{-1}u_{3})+\bm{\theta}
,
{\bm{u}}=(u_{1}\ u_{2}\ u_{3})
and
\bar{U}_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[j]}
denotes the conjugate of
U_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[j]}
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\left({\rm E}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-\widetilde{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})\right|^{p/2}\right)^{2/p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\widehat{K}_{1}(u_{1})\widehat{K}_{2}(u_{2})\widehat{K}_{3}(u_{3})|\left({\rm E}\left|\left(U_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[i]}(\bm{\theta}^{*}_{{\bm{u}}})-\widetilde{U}_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[i]}(\bm{\theta}^{*}_{{\bm{u}}})\right)\bar{U}_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[j]}(\bm{\theta}^{*}_{{\bm{u}}})\right|^{p/2}\right)^{2/p}{\rm d}{\bm{u}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\widehat{K}_{1}(u_{1})\widehat{K}_{2}(u_{2})\widehat{K}_{3}(u_{3})|\left({\rm E}\left|\widetilde{U}_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[i]}(\bm{\theta}^{*}_{{\bm{u}}})\left(\bar{U}_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[j]}(\bm{\theta}^{*}_{{\bm{u}}})-\bar{\widetilde{U}}_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[j]}(\bm{\theta}^{*}_{{\bm{u}}})\right)\right|^{p/2}\right)^{2/p}{\rm d}{\bm{u}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\widehat{K}_{1}(u_{1})\widehat{K}_{2}(u_{2})\widehat{K}_{3}(u_{3})|C_{p}^{2}\left(\varphi_{{\bm{m}}+{\bm{1}},p}^{[i]}\varphi_{{\bm{0}},p}^{[j]}+\varphi_{{\bm{0}},p}^{[i]}\varphi_{{\bm{m}}+{\bm{1}},p}^{[j]}\right){\rm d}{\bm{u}},

\displaystyle\leq A_{9}\left(\varphi_{{\bm{m}}+{\bm{1}},p}^{[i]}\varphi_{{\bm{0}},p}^{[j]}+\varphi_{{\bm{0}},p}^{[i]}\varphi_{{\bm{m}}+{\bm{1}},p}^{[j]}\right).

Now, combining all the results from above, we have

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\left({\rm E}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-{\rm E}\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})\right|^{p/2}\right)^{2/p}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\left[\left({\rm E}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-\widetilde{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})\right|^{p/2}\right)^{2/p}+|{\rm E}\left(\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-\widetilde{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})\right)|+\left({\rm E}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-{\rm E}\widetilde{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})\right|^{p/2}\right)^{2/p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq A_{10}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\varphi_{{\bm{m}}+{\bm{1}},p}^{[i]}+A_{8}\left(m_{1}^{2}m_{2}^{2}m_{3}^{2}B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}/\mathcal{R}\right)^{1/2}.

Under Conditions
1
and
2
, there exists
A_{11}\in(0,\infty)
such that
\varphi_{{\bm{m}}+{\bm{1}},p}^{[i]}<A_{11}\rho_{1}^{m_{1}}\rho_{2}^{m_{2}}\rho_{3}^{m_{3}}
. Take
m_{1}=-\log_{\rho_{1}}(B_{S_{1}}^{a_{1}}),m_{2}=-\log_{\rho_{2}}(B_{S_{2}}^{a_{2}}),m_{3}=-\log_{\rho_{3}}(B_{T}^{a_{3}})
such that
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}>0
and
a_{1}>(1/b_{1}-1)/2,a_{2}>(1/b_{1}^{*}-1)/2,a_{3}>(1/b_{1}^{**}-1)/2
. This yields

\varphi_{{\bm{m}}+{\bm{1}},p}^{[i]}<A_{11}B_{S_{1}}^{-a_{1}}B_{S_{2}}^{-a_{2}}B_{T}^{-a_{3}}=o(B_{S_{1}}^{(1-1/b_{1})/2}B_{S_{2}}^{(1-1/b_{1}^{*})/2}B_{T}^{(1-1/b_{1}^{**})/2})=o((B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}/\mathcal{R})^{1/2}),

and
m_{1}m_{2}m_{3}=A_{12}\log(B_{S_{1}})\log(B_{S_{2}})\log(B_{T})
for a positive constant
A_{12}
.
Hence, there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\left({\rm E}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-{\rm E}\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})\right|^{p/2}\right)^{2/p}}{\left(\log(B_{S_{1}})\log(B_{S_{2}})\log(B_{T}))^{2}B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}/\mathcal{R}\right)^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A_{10}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\varphi_{{\bm{m}}+{\bm{1}},p}^{[i]}+A_{8}\left(m_{1}^{2}m_{2}^{2}m_{3}^{2}B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}/\mathcal{R}\right)^{1/2}}{A_{12}^{-1}\left(m_{1}^{2}m_{2}^{2}m_{3}^{2}B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}/\mathcal{R}\right)^{1/2}}<C.

This completes the proof.

D.2
Proof of Proposition
D.2

Noting that

{\rm E}|\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-\sigma_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})|^{2}\leq 2\left[{\rm E}|\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-{\rm E}\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})|^{2}+{\rm E}|{\rm E}\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-\sigma_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})|^{2}\right],

where, because of Proposition
D.1
when
p=4
, the first term on the right-hand side satisfies

\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}{\rm E}|\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-{\rm E}\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})|^{2}\leq C^{2}(\log(B_{S_{1}})\log(B_{S_{2}})\log(B_{T}))^{2}B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}/\mathcal{R},

(D.38)

it only remains to deal with the second term.

Since

\displaystyle{\rm E}\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}_{1}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}_{2}={\bm{1}}}^{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}_{1}-\bm{\varsigma}_{2}}^{y}K_{1}\left(\frac{s_{11}-s_{21}}{B_{S_{1}}}\right)K_{2}\left(\frac{s_{12}-s_{22}}{B_{S_{2}}}\right)K_{3}\left(\frac{t_{1}-t_{2}}{B_{T}}\right)e^{-i\left\langle\bm{\varsigma}_{1}-\bm{\varsigma}_{2},\bm{\theta}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}}\sum_{s_{1}=1-S_{1}}^{S_{1}-1}\sum_{s_{2}=1-S_{2}}^{S_{2}-1}\sum_{t=1-T}^{T-1}(S_{1}-|s_{1}|)(S_{2}-|s_{2}|)(T-|t|)\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}}K_{1}\left(\frac{s_{1}}{B_{S_{1}}}\right)K_{2}\left(\frac{s_{2}}{B_{S_{2}}}\right)K_{3}\left(\frac{t}{B_{T}}\right)e^{-i\left\langle\bm{\varsigma},\bm{\theta}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s_{1}=1-S_{1}}^{S_{1}-1}\sum_{s_{2}=1-S_{2}}^{S_{2}-1}\sum_{t=1-T}^{T-1}\left(1-\frac{|s_{1}|}{S_{1}}\right)\left(1-\frac{|s_{2}|}{S_{2}}\right)\left(1-\frac{|t|}{T}\right)\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}}K_{1}\left(\frac{s_{1}}{B_{S_{1}}}\right)K_{2}\left(\frac{s_{2}}{B_{S_{2}}}\right)K_{3}\left(\frac{t}{B_{T}}\right)e^{-i\left\langle\bm{\varsigma},\bm{\theta}\right\rangle},

we have

\displaystyle\left|\left({\rm E}\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-\sigma_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{s_{1}=1-S_{1}}^{S_{1}-1}\sum_{s_{2}=1-S_{2}}^{S_{2}-1}\sum_{t=1-T}^{T-1}\left(K_{1}\left(\frac{s_{1}}{B_{S_{1}}}\right)K_{2}\left(\frac{s_{2}}{B_{S_{2}}}\right)K_{3}\left(\frac{t}{B_{T}}\right)-1\right)\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}}e^{-i\left\langle\bm{\varsigma},\bm{\theta}\right\rangle}\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\sum_{s_{1}=1-S_{1}}^{S_{1}-1}\sum_{s_{2}=1-S_{2}}^{S_{2}-1}\sum_{t=1-T}^{T-1}\left[\left(1-\frac{|s_{1}|}{S_{1}}\right)\left(1-\frac{|s_{2}|}{S_{2}}\right)\left(1-\frac{|t|}{T}\right)-1\right]\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\times\left.K_{1}\left(\frac{s_{1}}{B_{S_{1}}}\right)K_{2}\left(\frac{s_{2}}{B_{S_{2}}}\right)K_{3}\left(\frac{t}{B_{T}}\right)e^{-i\left\langle\bm{\varsigma},\bm{\theta}\right\rangle}\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\sum_{|s_{1}|\geq S_{1}}\sum_{|s_{2}|\geq S_{2}}\sum_{|t|\geq T}\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}}e^{-i\left\langle\bm{\varsigma},\bm{\theta}\right\rangle}\right|

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{C}_{ij,\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[1]}(\bm{\theta})+\mathcal{C}_{ij,\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[2]}(\bm{\theta})+\mathcal{C}_{ij,\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[3]}(\bm{\theta}).

Under Assumptions
6.5
and Condition
3
, there exists some
A_{3},A_{4}>0
such that, for all
i,j
and
\bm{\theta}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{ij,\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[1]}(\bm{\theta})

\displaystyle\leq A_{3}\sum_{s_{1}=-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{s_{2}=-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{t=-\infty}^{\infty}\rho_{4}^{|s_{1}|}\rho_{5}^{|s_{2}|}\rho_{6}^{|t|}\left\{\left(\frac{|s_{1}|}{B_{S_{1}}}\right)^{\vartheta_{1}}+\left(\frac{|s_{2}|}{B_{S_{2}}}\right)^{\vartheta_{2}}+\left(\frac{|t|}{B_{T}}\right)^{\vartheta_{3}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\frac{|s_{1}|}{B_{S_{1}}}\right)^{\vartheta_{1}}\left(\frac{|s_{2}|}{B_{S_{2}}}\right)^{\vartheta_{2}}+\left(\frac{|s_{1}|}{B_{S_{1}}}\right)^{\vartheta_{1}}\left(\frac{|t|}{B_{T}}\right)^{\vartheta_{3}}+\left(\frac{|s_{2}|}{B_{S_{2}}}\right)^{\vartheta_{2}}\left(\frac{|t|}{B_{T}}\right)^{\vartheta_{3}}+\left(\frac{|s_{1}|}{B_{S_{1}}}\right)^{\vartheta_{1}}\left(\frac{|s_{2}|}{B_{S_{2}}}\right)^{\vartheta_{2}}\left(\frac{|t|}{B_{T}}\right)^{\vartheta_{3}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq A_{4}\left\{B_{S_{1}}^{-\vartheta_{1}}+B_{S_{2}}^{-\vartheta_{2}}+B_{T}^{-\vartheta_{3}}\right\}.

Moreover, there exist
A_{5},...,A_{13}>0
such that, for all
i,j
and
\bm{\theta}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{ij,\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[2]}(\bm{\theta})

\displaystyle\leq A_{5}\sum_{s_{1}=-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{s_{2}=-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{t=-\infty}^{\infty}\left(\frac{|s_{1}|}{S_{1}}+\frac{|s_{2}|}{S_{2}}+\frac{|t|}{T}+\frac{|s_{1}s_{2}|}{S_{1}S_{2}}+\frac{|s_{1}t|}{S_{1}T}+\frac{|s_{2}t|}{S_{2}T}+\frac{|s_{1}s_{2}t|}{S_{1}S_{2}T}\right)\rho_{4}^{|s_{1}|}\rho_{5}^{|s_{2}|}\rho_{6}^{|t|}

\displaystyle\leq A_{6}S_{1}^{-1}+A_{7}S_{2}^{-1}+A_{8}T^{-1}+A_{9}(S_{1}S_{2})^{-1}+A_{10}(S_{1}T)^{-1}+A_{11}(S_{2}T)^{-1}+A_{12}(S_{1}S_{2}T)^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq A_{13}\max(S_{1}^{-1},S_{2}^{-1},T^{-1})

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{ij,\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{[3]}(\bm{\theta})\leq A_{2}\sum_{|s_{1}|\geq S_{1}}\sum_{|s_{2}|\geq S_{2}}\sum_{|t|\geq T}\rho_{4}^{|s_{1}|}\rho_{5}^{|s_{2}|}\rho_{6}^{|t|}=o(\max(S_{1}^{-1},S_{2}^{-1},T^{-1})).

Therefore,

\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\left|{\rm E}\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})-\sigma_{ij}^{y}(\bm{\theta})\right|^{2}=O\left\{\max\left[B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}/\mathcal{R},B_{S_{1}}^{-2\vartheta_{1}},B_{S_{2}}^{-2\vartheta_{2}},B_{T}^{-2\vartheta_{3}}\right]\right\},

which, together with (
D.38
), completes the proof.

Appendix E
Proof of results of Section 6

E.1
Proof of Theorem
6.1

We first prove the following three propositions.

Proposition E.1
.

Let Assumption
3.1
,
3.2
,
6.1
, and
6.2
hold. Then, there exists a finite
C>0
such that
\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\lambda_{n1}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})\leq C
.

Proof.

Let
\sigma_{ij}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})
denote the cross-spectral density of
\xi_{i}
and
\xi_{j}
. Then, by Assumptions
6.1
and
6.2
, for all
j=1,\ldots,n
and all
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}|\sigma_{ij}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left|\beta_{i\ell}(\bm{\theta})\bar{\beta}_{j\ell}(\bm{\theta})\right|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\kappa_{3}=0}^{\infty}|\beta_{i\ell,\bm{\kappa}}|\right)\left(\sum_{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\kappa_{3}=0}^{\infty}|\bar{\beta}_{j\ell,\bm{\kappa}}|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\kappa_{3}=0}^{\infty}A^{\xi}_{i\ell}{\rho_{1}^{\xi|\kappa_{1}|}}{\rho_{2}^{\xi|\kappa_{2}|}}{\rho_{3}^{\xi\kappa_{3}}}\right)\left(\sum_{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\kappa_{3}=0}^{\infty}A^{\xi}_{j\ell}{\rho_{1}^{\xi|\kappa_{1}|}}{\rho_{2}^{\xi|\kappa_{2}|}}{\rho_{3}^{\xi\kappa_{3}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(A^{\xi})^{2}C_{{\rho_{1}^{\xi}}{\rho_{2}^{\xi}}{\rho_{3}^{\xi}}},

for some finite
C_{{\rho_{1}^{\xi}}{\rho_{2}^{\xi}}{\rho_{3}^{\xi}}}>0
depending only on
{\rho_{1}^{\xi}}
,
{\rho_{2}^{\xi}}
, and
{\rho_{3}^{\xi}}
. This yields that

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\max_{j=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\sigma_{ij}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})|\leq\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\max_{j=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}|\sigma_{ij}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})|\leq(A^{\xi})^{2}C_{{\rho_{1}^{\xi}}{\rho_{2}^{\xi}}{\rho_{3}^{\xi}}}.

Let
\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})
be the spectral density matrix of
\bm{\xi}_{n}
. By Hölder inequality, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and all
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}
,

[\lambda_{n1}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})]^{2}=\|\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})\|^{2}\leq\|\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})\|_{1}\|\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})\|_{\infty}=\|\bm{\Sigma}_{n}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})\|_{1}^{2}=\left[\max_{j=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\sigma_{ij}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})|\right]^{2},

where
\|\cdot\|
denotes the spectral norm,
\|\cdot\|_{F}
denotes the Frobenius norm, and
\|\cdot\|_{1}
and
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
denote the column-wise and row-wise norms, respectively.
It follows that,

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\lambda_{n1}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})\leq\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{j=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\sigma_{ij}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})|\leq(A^{\xi})^{2}C_{{\rho_{1}^{\xi}}{\rho_{2}^{\xi}}{\rho_{3}^{\xi}}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition E.2
.

Let Assumptions
3.1
,
6.1
,
6.2
, and
6.4
hold. Then, for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, there exist a finite
p>4
, and
\widetilde{\rho}_{1},\widetilde{\rho}_{2},\widetilde{\rho}_{3}\in(0,1)
, and
\widetilde{A}_{1},\widetilde{A}_{2}>0
, such that

(i)

{\rm E}\left(|x_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|^{p}\right)\leq\widetilde{A}_{1}
;

(ii)

\delta^{[\ell]}_{\bm{\varsigma},p}({\bm{x}})\leq\widetilde{A}_{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{1}^{|s_{1}|}\widetilde{\rho}_{2}^{|s_{2}|}\widetilde{\rho}_{3}^{|t|}.

Proof.

The Minkowski inequality yields

\left\{{\rm E}\left(|x_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|^{p}\right)\right\}^{1/p}\leq\left\{{\rm E}\left(|\chi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|^{p}\right)\right\}^{1/p}+\left\{{\rm E}\left(|\xi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|^{p}\right)\right\}^{1/p},

so it suffices to bound the two terms on the RHS. Using again the Minkowski inequality and Assumptions
6.1
,
6.2
and
6.4
, there is a constant
\widetilde{A}_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left\{{\rm E}\left(|\xi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|^{p}\right)\right\}^{1/p}

\displaystyle=\left\{{\rm E}\left(\left|\sum_{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\kappa_{3}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\beta_{\ell j,\bm{\kappa}}\varepsilon_{j,\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\kappa}}\right|^{p}\right)\right\}^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\kappa_{3}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left\{{\rm E}\left(\left|\beta_{\ell j,\bm{\kappa}}\varepsilon_{j,\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\kappa}}\right|^{p}\right)\right\}^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\kappa_{3}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}|\beta_{\ell j,\bm{\kappa}}|\left\{{\rm E}\left(\left|\varepsilon_{j,\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\kappa}}\right|^{p}\right)\right\}^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq A^{\xi}\widetilde{A}_{{\rho_{1}^{\xi}}{\rho_{2}^{\xi}}{\rho_{3}^{\xi}}},

for some finite
\widetilde{A}_{{\rho_{1}^{\xi}}{\rho_{2}^{\xi}}{\rho_{3}^{\xi}}}>0
depending only on
{\rho_{1}^{\xi}}
,
{\rho_{2}^{\xi}}
, and
{\rho_{3}^{\xi}}
.
Similarly, we have
\left\{{\rm E}\left(|\chi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|^{p}\right)\right\}^{1/p}\leq{A}^{\chi}\widetilde{A}_{{\rho_{1}^{\chi}}{\rho_{2}^{\chi}}{\rho_{3}^{\chi}}}

for some finite
\widetilde{A}_{{\rho_{1}^{\chi}}{\rho_{2}^{\chi}}{\rho_{3}^{\chi}}}>0
depending only on
{\rho_{1}^{\chi}}
,
{\rho_{2}^{\chi}}
, and
{\rho_{3}^{\chi}}
.
Letting
\widetilde{A}_{1}=(A^{\xi}\widetilde{A}_{{\rho_{1}^{\xi}}{\rho_{2}^{\xi}}{\rho_{3}^{\xi}}}+{A}^{\chi}\widetilde{A}_{{\rho_{1}^{\chi}}{\rho_{2}^{\chi}}{\rho_{3}^{\chi}}})^{p}
yields
{\rm E}\left(|x_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|^{p}\right)\leq\widetilde{A}_{1}
. This proves part (i).

Turning to part (ii), for
t<0
, the inequality holds trivially since
\delta^{[\ell]}_{\bm{\varsigma},p}({\bm{x}})=0
. For
t\geq 0
,
notice that

\displaystyle\delta^{[\ell]}_{\bm{\varsigma},p}({\bm{x}})

\displaystyle=\{{\rm E}|x_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}-x^{*}_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|^{p}\}^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq\{{\rm E}|\chi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}-\chi^{*}_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|^{p}\}^{1/p}+\{{\rm E}|\xi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}-\xi^{*}_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|^{p}\}^{1/p}

\displaystyle=\delta^{[\ell]}_{\bm{\varsigma},p}(\bm{\chi})+\delta^{[\ell]}_{\bm{\varsigma},p}(\bm{\xi}).

Assumptions
6.1
,
6.2
and
6.4
entail that

\displaystyle\delta^{[\ell]}_{\bm{\varsigma},p}(\bm{\xi})

\displaystyle=\left\{{\rm E}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\beta_{\ell j,\bm{\varsigma}}(\varepsilon_{j,{\bm{0}}}-\varepsilon_{j,{\bm{0}}}^{*})\right|^{p}\right\}^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}|\beta_{\ell j,\bm{\varsigma}}|\left\{{\rm E}\left|(\varepsilon_{j,{\bm{0}}}-\varepsilon_{j,{\bm{0}}}^{*})\right|^{p}\right\}^{1/p}\leq\bar{A}A^{\xi}{\rho_{1}^{\xi}}^{|s_{1}|}{\rho_{2}^{\xi}}^{|s_{2}|}{\rho_{3}^{\xi}}^{t}.

Similarly, we have that
\delta^{[\ell]}_{\bm{\varsigma},p}(\bm{\chi})\leq\bar{A}A^{\chi}{\rho_{1}^{\chi}}^{|s_{1}|}{\rho_{2}^{\chi}}^{|s_{2}|}{\rho_{3}^{\chi}}^{t}
. The result then follows by letting

\widetilde{A}_{2}=\bar{A}\max(A^{\chi},A^{\xi})

and
\widetilde{\rho}_{h}=\max({\rho_{h}^{\xi}},{\rho_{h}^{\chi}})
,
h=1,2,3
.
∎

Proposition E.3
.

Let Assumptions
3.1
,
6.1
,
6.2
, and
6.4
hold. Then, letting
\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}}^{x}={\rm E}(x_{i\bm{\varsigma}}x_{j{\bm{0}}})
, for all
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
, there exist finite
\widetilde{\rho}_{4},\widetilde{\rho}_{5},\widetilde{\rho}_{6}\in(0,1)
and
\widetilde{A}^{*}>0
, such that
|\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}}^{x}|\leq\widetilde{A}^{*}\widetilde{\rho}_{4}^{|s_{1}|}\widetilde{\rho}_{5}^{|s_{2}|}\widetilde{\rho}_{6}^{|t|}
.

Proof.

We have
|\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}}^{x}|=|\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}}^{\chi}+\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}}^{\xi}|\leq|\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}}^{\chi}|+|\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}}^{\xi}|
. Since
\{{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{\bm{\varsigma}}=(\varepsilon_{1\bm{\varsigma}}\ \varepsilon_{2\bm{\varsigma}}\ \cdots)^{\top},\bm{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\}
is an i.i.d. infinite dimensional zero-mean orthonormal rf, by Assumptions
6.1
and
6.2
we have

\displaystyle|\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}}^{\xi}|

\displaystyle=\left|{\rm E}\left\{\left(\sum_{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\kappa_{3}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\beta_{ih,\bm{\kappa}}\varepsilon_{h,\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\kappa}}\right)\left(\sum_{\tilde{\kappa}_{1},\tilde{\kappa}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\tilde{\kappa}_{3}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\tilde{h}=1}^{\infty}\beta_{j\tilde{h},\tilde{\bm{\kappa}}}\varepsilon_{\tilde{h},-\tilde{\bm{\kappa}}}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|{\rm E}\left\{\left(\sum_{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\kappa_{3}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\beta_{ih,\bm{\kappa}}\beta_{jh,{\bm{\kappa}}-\bm{\varsigma}}\varepsilon_{h,\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\kappa}}^{2}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\kappa_{3}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\left|\beta_{ih,\bm{\kappa}}\beta_{jh,{\bm{\kappa}}-\bm{\varsigma}}\right|{\rm E}(\varepsilon_{h,\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\kappa}}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\kappa_{3}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}A_{ih}^{\xi}A_{jh}^{\xi}\rho_{1}^{\xi,|\kappa_{1}|+|\kappa_{1}-s_{1}|}\rho_{2}^{\xi,|\kappa_{2}|+|\kappa_{2}-s_{2}|}\rho_{3}^{\xi,\kappa_{3}+\kappa_{3}-t}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}A_{ih}^{\xi}A_{jh}^{\xi}\sum_{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\kappa_{3}=0}^{\infty}\rho_{1}^{\xi,2|\kappa_{1}|}\rho_{2}^{\xi,2|\kappa_{2}|}\rho_{3}^{\xi,2\kappa_{3}}\right\}{\rho^{\xi s_{1}|}_{1}}{\rho_{2}^{\xi|s_{2}|}}{\rho^{\xi}_{3}}^{|t|}

\displaystyle\leq(A^{\xi})^{2}\widetilde{A}^{*}_{\rho_{1}^{\xi},\rho_{2}^{\xi},\rho_{3}^{\xi}}{\rho^{\xi s_{1}|}_{1}}{\rho_{2}^{\xi|s_{2}|}}{\rho^{\xi}_{3}}^{|t|}

for some finite
\widetilde{A}^{*}_{\rho_{1}^{\xi},\rho_{2}^{\xi},\rho_{3}^{\xi}}>0
depending only on
{\rho_{1}^{\xi}}
,
{\rho_{2}^{\xi}}
, and
{\rho_{3}^{\xi}}
.

Similarly, there exists a finite
\widetilde{A}^{*}_{\rho_{1}^{\chi},\rho_{2}^{\chi},\rho_{3}^{\chi}}>0
depending only on
{\rho_{1}^{\chi}}
,
{\rho_{2}^{\chi}}
, and
{\rho_{3}^{\chi}}
, such that

|\Gamma_{ij,\bm{\varsigma}}^{\chi}|\leq\widetilde{A}^{*}_{\rho_{1}^{\chi},\rho_{2}^{\chi},\rho_{3}^{\chi}}{\rho^{\chi}_{1}}^{|s_{1}|}{\rho^{\chi}_{2}}^{|s_{2}|}{\rho^{\chi}_{3}}^{|t|}
. Letting

\widetilde{A}^{*}=\max(\widetilde{A}^{*}_{\rho_{1}^{\chi},\rho_{2}^{\chi},\rho_{3}^{\chi}},\widetilde{A}^{*}_{\rho_{1}^{\xi},\rho_{2}^{\xi},\rho_{3}^{\xi}})
and

\widetilde{\rho}_{4}=\max({\rho_{1}^{\xi}},{\rho^{\chi}_{1}})
,
\widetilde{\rho}_{5}=\max({\rho^{\xi}_{2}},{\rho^{\chi}_{2}})
, and
\widetilde{\rho}_{6}=\max({\rho^{\xi}_{3}},{\rho^{\chi}_{3}})
yields the desired result.
∎

It immediately follows from Assumption
3.1
and Proposition
E.2
, that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
each component
x_{\ell}
,
\ell=1,\ldots,n
of the rf
\bm{x}_{n}
satisfies Conditions
1
and
2
in Appendix
D
. Moreover, by Proposition
E.3
, we have that also Condition
3
is satisfied by all couples
x_{i}
and
x_{j}
,
i,j=1,\ldots,n
.
Therefore, we can apply Propositions
D.1

and

D.2
to the entries of the estimator of the spectral density matrix of
\bm{x}_{n}
, defined in (
17
). This yields the desired result.

E.2
Proof of Theorem
6.2

Hereafter, let

\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}=\max\left\{\frac{(\log B_{S_{1}}\log B_{S_{2}}\log B_{T})^{2}B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}}{S_{1}S_{2}T},\frac{1}{B_{S_{1}}^{2\vartheta_{1}}},\frac{1}{B_{S_{2}}^{2\vartheta_{2}}},\frac{1}{B_{T}^{2\vartheta_{3}}}\right\}.

Moreover, we let
C_{1},C_{2},\ldots
and
C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*},\ldots
denote generic finite positive constants independent of
n
. We let also
\bm{e}_{ni}
,
i=1,\ldots,n,
denote the
i
-th canonical basis of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Throughout, we make use of the spectral norm, denoted as
\|\cdot\|
, and the Frobenius norm, denoted as
\|\cdot\|_{F}
.
The proof relies on some lemmas introduced below.

Lemma E.4
.

Let Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
,
6.1
,
6.2
,
6.4
, and
6.5
hold. Then, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

(i)

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}n^{-2}{\rm E}\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right\|^{2}\leq C\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}
, where
C
is the same constant as in Theorem
6.1
;

(ii)

\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}n^{-1}{\rm E}\left\|\bm{e}_{ni}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right)\right\|^{2}\leq C\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}
, where
C
is the same constant as in Theorem
6.1
;

(iii)

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}n^{-1}\left\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})\right\|\leq C_{1}^{*}n^{-1}
;

(iv)

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}n^{-2}{\rm E}\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})\right\|^{2}\leq C_{1}\max(n^{-2},\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}})
;

(v)

\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}n^{-1}{\rm E}\left\|\bm{e}_{ni}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})\right)\right\|^{2}\leq C_{2}\max(n^{-1},\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}})
.

Proof.

Part (i). For any
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}
and any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right\|^{2}_{F}

\displaystyle={\rm trace}\left[\left(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right)^{\dagger}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right)\right]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-\sigma_{ij}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right|^{2}.

The result then follows from Theorem
6.1
.

Part (ii). Noticing that

n^{-1}{\rm E}\left\|\bm{e}_{ni}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right)\right\|^{2}=n^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\rm E}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-\sigma_{ij}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right|^{2},

the result then follows from Theorem
6.1
.

Part (iii). Since

\left\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})\right\|=\left\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})\right\|
, the result follows immediately from Proposition
E.1
.

Part (iv). Denote by
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})
the
n\times n
spectral density matrix of
{\bm{\xi}}_{n}
. We have

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-\left({\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})\right)\right\|\leq\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right\|+\left\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})\right\|.

The result then follows immediately from part (i) and Proposition
E.1
.

Part (v). Note that

\left\|\bm{e}_{ni}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})\right)\right\|\leq\left\|\bm{e}_{ni}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right)\right\|+\left\|\bm{e}_{ni}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\xi}(\bm{\theta})\right\|.

The result then follows immediately from part (ii) and Proposition
E.1
.
∎

Lemma E.5
.

Let Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
,
6.1
,
6.2
,
6.4
, and
6.5
hold. Then, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and all
j=1,\ldots,q
,

(i)

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}n^{-2}{\rm E}|\widehat{\lambda}_{nj}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-\lambda_{nj}^{x}(\bm{\theta})|^{2}\leq C\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}
, where
C
is the same constant as in Lemma
E.4
(i);

(ii)

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}n^{-1}|{\lambda}_{nj}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-\lambda_{nj}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})|\leq C_{1}^{*}n^{-1}
, where
C_{1}^{*}
is the same constant as in Lemma
E.4
(iii);

(iii)

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}n^{-2}{\rm E}|\widehat{\lambda}_{nj}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-\lambda_{nj}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})|^{2}\leq C_{1}\max(n^{-2},\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}),
where
C_{1}
is the same constant as in Lemma
E.4
(iv);

Proof.

For any two
n\times n
matrices
\bm{A}_{1}
and
\bm{A}_{2}
, Wely’s inequality (see Appendix
B.1
) implies that

|\nu_{\ell}(\bm{A}_{1}+\bm{A}_{2})-\nu_{\ell}(\bm{A}_{1})|\leq\|\bm{A}_{2}\|,\ell=1,\ldots,n.

(E.39)

Part (i). Letting
\bm{A}_{1}={\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})
,
\bm{A}_{2}=\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})
,
the result then follows from (
E.39
), which implies
|\widehat{\lambda}_{nj}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-\lambda_{nj}^{x}(\bm{\theta})|\leq\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\|

and Lemma
E.4
(i).

Part (ii). Letting

\bm{A}_{1}={\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})
,
\bm{A}_{2}={\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})
yields, because of (
E.39
),

|{\lambda}_{nj}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-\lambda_{nj}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})|\leq\left\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})\right\|.
The result hence follows from Lemma
E.4
(iii).

Part (iii). Letting
\bm{A}_{1}={\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})
,
\bm{A}_{2}=\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})
,
the result then follows from (
E.39
), which implies

|\widehat{\lambda}_{nj}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-\lambda_{nj}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})|\leq\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})\|

and Lemma
E.4
(iv).
∎

Lemma E.6
.

Let Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
,

6.2
, and
6.3

hold. Then, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\left\|\bm{e}_{ni}^{\top}n^{1/2}({{\bm{P}}^{\chi}_{n}}(\bm{\theta}))^{\dagger}\right\|\leq C_{2}.

Proof.

Let
{p}_{n,jk}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})
,
j=1,\ldots,q
,
k=1,\ldots,n,
denote
(j,k)
-th entry of
{\bm{P}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})
.
Then,

\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\sigma^{\chi}_{kk}(\bm{\theta})=\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\lambda_{nj}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})|{p}_{n,jk}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})|^{2}<\infty.

Indeed, by Assumptions
6.1
and
6.2

\sigma^{\chi}_{kk}(\bm{\theta})=\sum_{\bm{h}}e^{-i\langle\bm{h},\bm{\theta}\rangle}\text{E}[\chi_{k\bm{\varsigma}}\chi_{k\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{h}}]\leq\sum_{\bm{h}}|\text{E}[\chi_{k\bm{\varsigma}}\chi_{k\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{h}}]|\leq\sum_{h_{1}}\sum_{h_{2}}\sum_{h_{3}}\widetilde{A}^{*}_{\rho_{1}^{\chi},\rho_{2}^{\chi},\rho_{3}^{\chi}}{\rho^{\chi}_{1}}^{|h_{1}|}{\rho^{\chi}_{2}}^{|h_{2}|}{\rho^{\chi}_{3}}^{|h_{3}|}<\infty,

where
\widetilde{A}^{*}_{\rho_{1}^{\chi},\rho_{2}^{\chi},\rho_{3}^{\chi}}
,
\rho^{\chi}_{1},\rho^{\chi}_{2}
, and
\rho^{\chi}_{3}
are defined in the proof of Proposition
E.3
. Moreover,
\sigma^{\chi}_{kk}(\bm{\theta})\geq 0

(
Brockwell and Davis 2006
, Corollary 4.3.2, p.120)
.

Now, since, by Assumption
6.3
, for all
j=1,\ldots,q
,

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\frac{\lambda_{nj}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})}{n}>\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\undertilde{\omega}(\bm{\theta})_{j}>0,

we must have
\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}n|{p}_{n,jk}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})|^{2}<C_{2}
, where
C_{2}
is independent of
j
and
k
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma E.7
.

Let Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
,

6.2
, and
6.3

hold. Then, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

For all
j=1,\ldots,q-1
there exist continuous functions

\bm{\theta}\mapsto\widetilde{\omega}_{j}^{*}(\bm{\theta})
and
\bm{\theta}\mapsto\undertilde{\omega}_{j}^{*}(\bm{\theta})

such that for all
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}

0<\undertilde{\omega}_{j+1}^{*}(\bm{\theta})\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_{n,j+1}^{x}(\bm{\theta})}{n}\leq\widetilde{\omega}_{j+1}^{*}(\bm{\theta})<\undertilde{\omega}_{j}^{*}(\bm{\theta})\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_{nj}^{x}(\bm{\theta})}{n}\leq\widetilde{\omega}_{j}^{*}(\bm{\theta})<\infty.

Proof.

The result follows immediately from Lemma
E.5
(ii) and Assumption
6.3
.
∎

Lemma E.8
.

Let Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
,
6.1
,
6.2
,
6.3
,
6.4
,
6.5
, and
6.6
hold. Then, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\max_{1\leq\ell\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}{\rm E}\left\|\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}n^{1/2}\left[{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n}(\bm{\theta})-\widehat{{\bm{K}}}^{x}_{n}(\bm{\theta})\right]\right\|^{2}\leq C_{3}^{*}\max(n^{-1},\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}).

Proof.

Theorem 2 in
Yu, Wang and Samworth 2015
implies that there exists a
q\times q
orthogonal matrix
{\bm{O}}_{q}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{P}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{O}}_{q}\widehat{\bm{P}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bm{P}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{O}}_{q}\widehat{\bm{P}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}^{*}\frac{\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})\right\|}{{\lambda}_{nq}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})-{\lambda}_{n,q+1}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})}\leq C_{5}^{*}n^{-1}\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})\right\|,

(E.40)

where we used the fact that
{\lambda}_{n,q+1}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})=0
and the last inequality is due to Lemma
E.7
. Note that

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}n^{1/2}\left[{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n}(\bm{\theta})-\widehat{{\bm{K}}}^{x}_{n}(\bm{\theta})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}n^{1/2}{\bm{P}}_{n}^{\chi{\dagger}}(\bm{\theta})\left[{\bm{P}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{O}}_{q}\widehat{\bm{P}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right]+\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}n^{1/2}\left[{\bm{P}}_{n}^{\chi{\dagger}}(\bm{\theta}){\bm{O}}_{q}-\widehat{\bm{P}}_{n}^{x{\dagger}}(\bm{\theta})\right]\widehat{\bm{P}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,{\bm{d}}_{n\ell}^{(1)}(\bm{\theta})+{\bm{d}}_{n\ell}^{(2)}(\bm{\theta}),\hskip 18.49988pt\text{say}.

For
{\bm{d}}_{n\ell}^{(1)}(\bm{\theta})
,
in view of (
E.2
) and Lemmas
E.4
(iv) and
E.6
, we have

\max_{1\leq\ell\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}{\rm E}\left\|{\bm{d}}_{n\ell}^{(1)}(\bm{\theta})\right\|^{2}\leq C_{6}^{*}\max(n^{-2},\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}).

As for
{\bm{d}}_{n\ell}^{(2)}(\bm{\theta})
, note that

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left\|n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left[{\bm{P}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{O}}_{q}\widehat{\bm{P}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right]\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|n^{1/2}\left[{\bm{P}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{O}}_{q}\widehat{\bm{P}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right]\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(C_{5}^{*})^{2}n^{-2}\left\|n^{1/2}\left[\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})\right]\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}

\displaystyle=(C_{5}^{*})^{2}n^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left\|\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left[\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})\right]\right\|^{2}.

Hence, for all
\ell=1,\ldots,n
,
{\rm E}\left\|n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left[{\bm{P}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{O}}_{q}\widehat{\bm{P}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})\right]\right\|^{2}
is of order no greater than

\max_{1\leq i\leq n}n^{-1}{\rm E}\left\|\bm{e}_{ni}^{\top}\left[\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{x}(\bm{\theta})-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{n}^{\chi}(\bm{\theta})\right]\right\|^{2},

which, in view of Lemma
E.4
(v), entails

\max_{1\leq\ell\leq n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}{\rm E}\left\|{\bm{d}}_{n\ell}^{(2)}(\bm{\theta})\right\|^{2}\leq C_{7}^{*}\max(n^{-1},\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}).

The result then follows.
∎

We can then prove the theorem as follows.
Let
\bm{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}=(\chi_{1\bm{\varsigma}},\ldots,\chi_{n\bm{\varsigma}})^{\top}
and
\bm{\xi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}=(\xi_{1\bm{\varsigma}},\ldots,\xi_{n\bm{\varsigma}})^{\top}
.
Recall from (
16
) in Remark
5.1
that

\displaystyle{\underline{{\bm{K}}}}^{\chi}_{n}(L){\bm{x}}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}={\underline{{\bm{K}}}}^{\chi}_{n}(L)\bm{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}+{\underline{{\bm{K}}}}^{\chi}_{n}(L)\bm{\xi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}={\underline{{\bm{K}}}}^{\chi}_{n}(L)\bm{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}=\bm{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}}.

Letting

\mathcal{D}_{{\bm{\varsigma}}}=\{(s_{1},s_{2},t):\underline{\kappa}_{1}(s_{1})\leq s_{1}\leq\overline{\kappa}_{1}(s_{1}),\underline{\kappa}_{2}(s_{2})\leq s_{2}\leq\overline{\kappa}_{2}(s_{2}),\underline{\kappa}_{3}(t)\leq t\leq\overline{\kappa}_{3}(t)\},

we then have

\displaystyle\widehat{\chi}^{(n)}_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}-\chi^{n}_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}_{{\bm{\varsigma}}}}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\underline{{\bm{K}}}}^{x}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}-\underline{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}\right){\bm{x}}_{n\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}+\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{{\bm{\varsigma}}}}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\underline{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}{\bm{x}}_{n\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}={a}_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}+\widetilde{a}_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}},\;\text{say}.

We first derive convergence rate of
{a}_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}
.
Letting
\bm{C}_{n\bm{\kappa}}
be the
n\times q
matrix with entries
c_{\ell j,\bm{\kappa}}
,
\ell=1,\ldots,n
,
j=1,\ldots,q
,
\bm{\kappa}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}
, from Assumption
6.1
, we have

\bm{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}=\sum_{\bm{\kappa}}\bm{C}_{n\bm{\kappa}}\bm{v}_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}-\bm{\kappa}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle|{a}_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}_{{\bm{\varsigma}}}}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\underline{{\bm{K}}}}^{x}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}-\underline{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}\right)\left(\sum_{\bm{\kappa}}\bm{C}_{n\bm{\kappa}}\bm{v}_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}-\bm{\kappa}}\right)\right|+\left|\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}_{{\bm{\varsigma}}}}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\underline{{\bm{K}}}}^{x}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}-\underline{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}\right)\bm{\xi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}\right|=a_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{(1)}+a_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{(2)},\;\text{say}.

Consider
a_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{(1)}
. By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle{\rm E}(a_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{(1)})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}_{{\bm{\varsigma}}}}{\rm E}\left|\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\underline{{\bm{K}}}}^{x}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}-\underline{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}\right)\left(\sum_{\bm{\kappa}}\bm{C}_{n\bm{\kappa}}\bm{v}_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}-\bm{\kappa}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}_{{\bm{\varsigma}}}}\left\{{\rm E}\left\|n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\underline{{\bm{K}}}}^{x}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}-\underline{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}\right)\right\|^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\left\{n^{-1}{\rm E}\left\|\sum_{\bm{\kappa}}\bm{C}_{n\bm{\kappa}}\bm{v}_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}-\bm{\kappa}}\right\|^{2}\right\}^{1/2}.

(E.41)

Now, because of Assumption
6.1
, which implies that
\bm{v}
is a white noise rf, and Assumption
6.2

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}{\rm E}\left\|\sum_{\bm{\kappa}}\bm{C}_{n\bm{\kappa}}\bm{v}_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}-\bm{\kappa}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}{\rm E}\left\|\sum_{\bm{\kappa}}\bm{C}_{n\bm{\kappa}}\bm{v}_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}-\bm{\kappa}}\right\|^{2}_{F}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\bm{\kappa}}\sum_{\bm{\kappa}^{\prime}}{\rm trace}\left(\bm{C}_{n\bm{\kappa}}{\rm E}(\bm{v}_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}-\bm{\kappa}}\bm{v}_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}-\bm{\kappa}^{\prime}}^{\dagger})\bm{C}_{n\bm{\kappa}^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\bm{\kappa}}{\rm trace}\left(\bm{C}_{n\bm{\kappa}}\bm{C}_{n\bm{\kappa}}^{\dagger}\right)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\bm{\kappa}}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}|c_{\ell j,\bm{\kappa}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\bm{\kappa}}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(A^{\chi}_{\ell j}{\rho_{1}^{\chi|\kappa_{1}|}}{\rho_{2}^{\chi|\kappa_{2}|}}{\rho_{3}^{\chi\kappa_{3}}}\right)^{2}\leq q\max_{1\leq\ell\leq n}\max_{1\leq j\leq q}(A^{\chi}_{\ell j})^{2}\widetilde{A}_{{\rho_{1}^{\chi}}{\rho_{2}^{\chi}}{\rho_{3}^{\chi}}}

\displaystyle\leq q(A^{\chi})^{2}\widetilde{A}_{{\rho_{1}^{\chi}}{\rho_{2}^{\chi}}{\rho_{3}^{\chi}}}=C_{8}^{*},\;\text{say},

(E.42)

for some finite
\widetilde{A}_{{\rho_{1}^{\chi}}{\rho_{2}^{\chi}}{\rho_{3}^{\chi}}}>0
depending only on
{\rho_{1}^{\chi}}
,
{\rho_{2}^{\chi}}
, and
{\rho_{3}^{\chi}}
.
Furthermore, noting that

\displaystyle\left\|n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\underline{{\bm{K}}}}^{x}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}-\underline{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{8\pi^{3}}\int_{\bm{\Theta}}n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left[\widehat{{\bm{K}}}^{x}_{n}(\bm{\theta})-{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n}(\bm{\theta})\right]e^{i\langle\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime},\bm{\theta}\rangle}{\rm d}\bm{\theta}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8\pi^{3}}\int_{\bm{\Theta}}\left\|n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left[\widehat{{\bm{K}}}^{x}_{n}(\bm{\theta})-{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n}(\bm{\theta})\right]\right\|{\rm d}\bm{\theta},

we have

\displaystyle\left\|n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\underline{{\bm{K}}}}^{x}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}-\underline{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}\right)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{8\pi^{3}}\right)^{2}\left\{\int_{\bm{\Theta}}\left\|n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left[\widehat{{\bm{K}}}^{x}_{n}(\bm{\theta})-{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n}(\bm{\theta})\right]\right\|{\rm d}\bm{\theta}\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8\pi^{3}}\int_{\bm{\Theta}}\left\|n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left[\widehat{{\bm{K}}}^{x}_{n}(\bm{\theta})-{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n}(\bm{\theta})\right]\right\|^{2}{\rm d}\bm{\theta},

which, because of Lemma
E.8
, entails that

\displaystyle{\rm E}\left\|n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\underline{{\bm{K}}}}^{x}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}-\underline{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}\right)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8\pi^{3}}\int_{\bm{\Theta}}{\rm E}\left\|n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left[\widehat{{\bm{K}}}^{x}_{n}(\bm{\theta})-{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n}(\bm{\theta})\right]\right\|^{2}{\rm d}\bm{\theta}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}{\rm E}\left\|n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\left[\widehat{{\bm{K}}}^{x}_{n}(\bm{\theta})-{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n}(\bm{\theta})\right]\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}^{*}\max(n^{-1},\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}).

(E.43)

By using (
E.42
) and (
E.43
), into (
E.41
), and recalling (
18
) and (
19
), we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq\ell\leq n}{\rm E}(a_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{(1)})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C_{3}^{*}C_{8}^{*}}\max(n^{-1/2},\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{1/2})(\overline{\kappa}_{1}(s_{1})-\underline{\kappa}_{1}(s_{1}))(\overline{\kappa}_{2}(s_{2})-\underline{\kappa}_{2}(s_{2}))(\overline{\kappa}_{3}(t)-\underline{\kappa}_{3}(t))

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C_{3}^{*}C_{8}^{*}}\max(n^{-1/2},\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{1/2})M_{S_{1}}M_{S_{2}}M_{T}.

Applying the same arguments will yield the same convergence rate of
a_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{(2)}
. We hence have

\max_{1\leq\ell\leq n}{\rm E}(a_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}})\leq C\max(n^{-1/2},\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{1/2})M_{S_{1}}M_{S_{2}}M_{T},

for some finite
C>0
independent of
n,S_{1},S_{2}
and
T
.

Turning to
\widetilde{a}_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}
, we have

\displaystyle|\widetilde{a}_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{{\bm{\varsigma}}}}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\underline{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{\bm{\kappa}}\bm{C}_{n\bm{\kappa}}\bm{v}_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}-\bm{\kappa}}\right)\right|+\left|\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{{\bm{\varsigma}}}}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\underline{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}\bm{\xi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}\right|=\widetilde{a}_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{(1)}+\widetilde{a}_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{(2)},\;\text{say}.

Consider
\widetilde{a}_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{(1)}
. By repeating the same arguments for bounding
{\rm E}(a_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{(1)})
, and using Assumption
6.6
and Lemma
E.6
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm E}(\widetilde{a}_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{(1)})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{{\bm{\varsigma}}}}\left\{{\rm E}\left\|n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\underline{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}}\right\|^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\left\{n^{-1}{\rm E}\left\|\sum_{\bm{\kappa}}\bm{C}_{n\bm{\kappa}}\bm{v}_{\bm{\varsigma}-\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}-\bm{\kappa}}\right\|^{2}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C_{8}^{*}}\left\{{\rm E}\left\|n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\underline{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n(0\ 0\ 0)}\right\|^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\sum_{\bm{\varsigma}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{{\bm{\varsigma}}}}(1+\varepsilon_{1})^{-|s_{1}^{\prime}|}(1+\varepsilon_{2})^{-|s_{2}^{\prime}|}(1+\varepsilon_{3})^{-|t^{\prime}|}

\displaystyle\leq{C}_{9}^{*}\left\{{\rm E}\left\|n^{1/2}\bm{e}_{n\ell}^{\top}\underline{{\bm{K}}}^{\chi}_{n(0\ 0\ 0)}\right\|^{2}\right\}^{1/2}(1+\varepsilon_{1})^{-\kappa^{*}_{1}(s_{1})}(1+\varepsilon_{2})^{-\kappa^{*}_{2}(s_{2})}(1+\varepsilon_{3})^{-\kappa^{*}_{3}(t)}

\displaystyle\leq C^{*}(1+\varepsilon_{1})^{-\kappa^{*}_{1}(s_{1})}(1+\varepsilon_{2})^{-\kappa^{*}_{2}(s_{2})}(1+\varepsilon_{3})^{-\kappa^{*}_{3}(t)},

for some finite
C^{*}>0
independent of
\ell,n,S_{1},S_{2}
, and
T
.
Applying the same arguments will yield the same convergence rate of
\widetilde{a}_{n\ell\bm{\varsigma}}^{(2)}
.

All of the above arguments yield

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq\ell\leq n}{\rm E}\left|\widehat{\chi}^{(n)}_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}-\chi_{\ell\bm{\varsigma}}\right|\leq\

\displaystyle C\max(n^{-1/2},\alpha_{\bar{\bm{\varsigma}}}^{1/2})M_{S_{1}}M_{S_{2}}M_{T}+C^{*}(1+\varepsilon_{1})^{-\kappa^{*}_{1}(s_{1})}(1+\varepsilon_{2})^{-\kappa^{*}_{2}(s_{2})}(1+\varepsilon_{3})^{-\kappa^{*}_{3}(t)}.

This proves part (i).

Furthermore, we have that

(1+\varepsilon_{1})^{-\kappa^{*}_{1}(s_{1})}(1+\varepsilon_{2})^{-\kappa^{*}_{2}(s_{2})}(1+\varepsilon_{3})^{-\kappa^{*}_{3}(t)}=o\left\{(1+\varepsilon_{1})^{-M_{S_{1}}}(1+\varepsilon_{2})^{-M_{S_{2}}}(1+\varepsilon_{3})^{-M_{T}}\right\},

which by Assumption
6.5
is dominated by the first term of part (i). This proves part (ii) and completes the proof.

Appendix F
Proofs of Results of of Section 7

In order to prove the theorem, we need the following result

Lemma F.1
.

Let Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
,
6.1
,
6.2
,
6.3
,
6.4
,
6.5
, and
7.1
hold. Then, for all
\epsilon>0
there exist
\delta_{\epsilon}
,
S_{1\epsilon}
,
S_{2\epsilon}
and
T_{\epsilon}
such that for any fixed
q_{\max}
,
n
,
S_{1}>S_{1\epsilon}
,
S_{2}>S_{2\epsilon}
, and
T>T_{\epsilon}
,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq q_{\max}}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}{\rm P}\left\{\min\left[\frac{1}{\log B_{S_{1}}\log B_{S_{2}}\log B_{T}}\sqrt{\frac{S_{1}S_{2}T}{B_{S_{1}}B_{S_{2}}B_{T}}},B_{S_{1}}^{\vartheta_{1}},B_{S_{2}}^{\vartheta_{2}},B_{T}^{\vartheta_{3}}\right]\frac{|\widehat{\lambda}^{x}_{nk}(\bm{\theta})-{\lambda}^{x}_{nk}(\bm{\theta})|}{n}>\delta_{\epsilon}\right\}<\epsilon.

Proof.

The result follows immediately from Lemma A.1 in
Hallin and
Liška 2007
and Lemma
E.5
(i).
∎

It then suffices to prove that for all
k\neq q
,
0\leq k\leq q_{\max}
, as
n,S_{1},S_{2},T\to\infty
,

{\rm P}\left[\widehat{\rm IC}^{(n)}(k)-\widehat{\rm IC}^{(n)}(q)>0\right]\rightarrow 1.

(F.44)

Start with the case that
k<q
. Letting

\widehat{D}_{nk}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\frac{1}{8\pi^{3}}\int_{\bm{\Theta}}\widehat{\lambda}^{x}_{nj}(\bm{\theta})\mathrm{d}\bm{\theta}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{ and }\hskip 9.24994pt{D}_{nk}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\frac{1}{8\pi^{3}}\int_{\bm{\Theta}}{\lambda}^{x}_{nj}(\bm{\theta})\mathrm{d}\bm{\theta},

we have
